## Unified Operating Specification

**Version:** 1.1.0
**Status:** Planning Phase

## [TECH_STACK]

### Core Platform
| Component | Technology | Version | Purpose |
|-----------|------------|---------|---------|
| Trading Platform | MetaTrader 5 | Build 3000+ | Order Execution |
| Primary Language | MQL5 | 5.0 | EA Development |
| AI/ML Backend | Python | 3.11+ | Research & Intelligence |
| Web Framework | FastAPI | 0.100+ | API Gateway |
| Database | PostgreSQL | 15+ | Canonical State (proved on a real PG16 server 2026-09-25; **SQLite is the runtime default** — `POSTGRES_HOST` empty → `/system/health.database` reads *"SQLite persistence active"*) |
| Cache / live state | Redis | 7+ | Real-time State (optional: `TRADERAIG_STATE_REDIS=0` → in-process ring; Redis only mirrors the ops event feed) |
| Message Queue | RabbitMQ | 3.12+ | **NOT IMPLEMENTED — aspirational row, see [ORPHANS].** The delivered event bus is `operations/event_feed.py` (bounded in-process ring + resume-by-id) mirrored to Redis. No `pika`/`amqp` dependency exists; remove this row rather than let a dashboard imply a broker that is not there. |
| Containerization | Docker | 24+ | Deployment |

### MQL5 Libraries
| Library | Purpose |
|---------|---------|
| Trade.mqh | Order Management |
| Indicators/ | Technical Analysis |
| Files/ | Data Persistence |

### Python Libraries
| Library | Version | Purpose |
|---------|---------|---------|
| pandas | 2.0+ | Data Processing |
| numpy | 1.24+ | Numerical Computing |
| scikit-learn | 1.5+ | ML Models |
| pydantic | 2.0+ | Data Validation |
| sqlalchemy | 2.0+ | ORM + persistence |
| psycopg | 3.x | PostgreSQL driver |
| redis | 5+ | State sync / cache |
| fastapi | 0.100+ | API Gateway |
| uvicorn | 0.30+ | ASGI server |
| websockets | 11+ | Real-time Communication |
| dspy *(optional AI extra)* | 3.3+ | **AI Program Compiler** — programs, evaluation, self-optimization (`requirements-ai.txt`; lazy + explicitly gated, never on the execution path) |

## [SYSTEM_FLOW]

### Primary Decision Flow
```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                    DATA INGESTION LAYER                      │
│  MT5 Feed → Normalize → Validate → Store → Publish          │
└─────────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────┘
                              │
                              ▼
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                    REGIME DETECTION                          │
│  Market Structure → Trend → Volatility → Session → Snapshot │
└─────────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────┘
                              │
                              ▼
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                    EVIDENCE GATHERING                        │
│  Multi-Timeframe → School Analysis → Confluence → Score     │
└─────────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────┘
                              │
                              ▼
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                    THESIS CONSTRUCTION                       │
│  Hypothesis → Validation → Risk/Reward → Ranking            │
└─────────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────┘
                              │
                              ▼
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                    CANDIDATE BOARD                           │
│  Selection → Portfolio Fit → Priority → Approval            │
└─────────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────┘
                              │
                              ▼
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                    RISK GOVERNOR                             │
│  Position Sizing → Exposure Check → Drawdown → Veto/Approve │
└─────────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────┘
                              │
                              ▼
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                    EXECUTION ENGINE                          │
│  Order Construction → Routing → Fill Monitoring → Confirm   │
└─────────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────┘
                              │
                              ▼
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                    RECONCILIATION                            │
│  State Sync → Position Verify → PnL Calc → Audit Log       │
└─────────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────┘
                              │
                              ▼
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                    LEARNING LOOP                             │
│  Experience Record → Error Class → Replay → Model Update     │
│  Program Evolution (DSPy): optimize analysis programs from   │
│  validated outcomes → new candidate versions → validation lab │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

### Event Types
| Event | Source | Consumers |
|-------|--------|-----------|
| `MarketDataUpdated` | MT5 Feed | Regime, Evidence, Thesis |
| `RegimeChanged` | Regime Engine | Evidence, Thesis, Risk |
| `EvidenceGathered` | Evidence Engine | Thesis, Candidate |
| `ThesisConstructed` | Thesis Engine | Candidate, Risk |
| `CandidateSelected` | Candidate Board | Risk, Execution |
| `RiskDecisionMade` | Risk Governor | Execution, Audit |
| `OrderSubmitted` | Execution | Position, Audit |
| `OrderFilled` | MT5 | Position, Performance |
| `PositionClosed` | MT5 | Performance, Learning |
| `ErrorDetected` | Any | Audit, Learning |

┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                     TRADERAIG OPERATING CORE                     │
├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│                                                                  │
│  ┌────────────────┐  ┌────────────────┐  ┌────────────────┐     │
│  │   GOVERNANCE   │  │   RESEARCH &   │  │  INVESTMENT    │     │
│  │     CORE       │  │ INTELLIGENCE   │  │   DECISION     │     │
│  │                │  │     CORE       │  │     CORE       │     │
│  └────────┬───────┘  └────────┬───────┘  └────────┬───────┘     │
│           │                   │                   │              │

│  │                   CANONICAL STATE                       │     │
│  │  Market │ Account │ Position │ Order │ Risk │ Thesis   │     │

│                               │                                  │
│  ┌────────────────┐  ┌────────────────┐  ┌────────────────┐     │

│  │   EXECUTION    │  │  OPERATIONS    │  │   EVOLUTION    │     │
│  │     CORE       │  │     CORE       │  │     CORE       │     │

### Core 1: Governance Core
**Responsibility:** Identity, Subscriptions, Entitlements, Audit Trail

| Component | Function | Interface |
|-----------|----------|-----------|
| IdentityService | User/Account Management | CRUD + Auth |
| SubscriptionService | Plan Management | State Machine |
| EntitlementService | Permission Checks | Policy Engine |
| AuditService | Action Logging | Append-only Log |

**State Schema:**
```python
class UserState:
    user_id: str
    account_ids: List[str]
    subscription_tier: SubscriptionTier
    entitlements: Entitlements
    risk_policy: RiskPolicy
    created_at: datetime
    updated_at: datetime

class SubscriptionState:
    subscription_id: str
    user_id: str
    plan: SubscriptionPlan
    status: SubscriptionStatus
    expires_at: datetime
    payment_method: PaymentMethod
```

### Core 2: Research & Intelligence Core
**Responsibility:** Market Analysis, Regime Detection, Evidence Gathering

| Component | Function | Update Frequency |
|-----------|----------|------------------|
| RegimeEngine | Market Structure | On significant move |
| SchoolEngines | SMC/ICT Analysis | On candle close |
| EvidenceAggregator | Multi-source scoring | On new evidence |
| ContextClassifier | Session/Condition | On session change |

**Tiered Computation:**
```
Tier 0 (Deterministic): Price action basics, session detection
Tier 1 (Quantitative): Indicators, statistics, correlations
Tier 2 (AI/ML): Pattern recognition, regime classification
Tier 3 (Expert): Complex scenarios, edge cases only
```

**State Schema:**
```python
class RegimeSnapshot:
    timestamp: datetime
    trend: TrendDirection
    structure: MarketStructure
    volatility: VolatilityRegime
    session: TradingSession
    liquidity: LiquidityState
    confidence: float

class EvidenceSet:
    evidence_id: str
    timestamp: datetime
    regime_id: str
    signals: List[Signal]
    confluence_score: float
    conflicts: List[Conflict]
    metadata: Dict
```

### Core 3: Investment Decision Core
**Responsibility:** Thesis Construction, Candidate Selection, Portfolio Management

| Component | Function | Output |
|-----------|----------|--------|
| ThesisEngine | Hypothesis Creation | Thesis |
| CandidateBoard | Trade Selection | TradeCandidate |
| PortfolioOptimizer | Allocation | PositionSize |
| ScorerEngine | Multi-criteria Ranking | Score |

**State Schema:**
```python
class Thesis:
    thesis_id: str
    timestamp: datetime
    regime_id: str
    evidence_ids: List[str]
    direction: TradeDirection
    entry_zone: PriceRange
    stop_loss: Price
    take_profit: List[Price]
    invalidation: InvalidationCondition
    confidence: float
    risk_reward: float
    status: ThesisStatus

class TradeCandidate:
    candidate_id: str
    thesis_id: str
    symbol: str
    direction: TradeDirection
    entry: float
    stop_loss: float
    take_profit: float
    size: float
    risk_reward: float
    priority: int
    approval_status: ApprovalStatus
```

### Core 4: Risk & Execution Core
**Responsibility:** Risk Governance, Order Execution, Position Management

| Component | Function | Authority |
|-----------|----------|-----------|
| RiskGovernor | Veto/Approve | Override all |
| PositionSizer | Calculate Size | Within limits |
| ExecutionEngine | Order Routing | Post-approval |
| PositionManager | Track State | Real-time |
| ReconciliationEngine | Verify Truth | Continuous |

**Risk Limits (Hardcoded):**
```python
class RiskPolicy:
    max_risk_per_trade: float = 0.01  # 1%
    max_daily_loss: float = 0.03      # 3%
    max_drawdown: float = 0.10        # 10%
    max_open_positions: int = 5
    max_correlation: float = 0.7
    max_leverage: float = 10.0
    require_stop_loss: bool = True
```

**State Schema:**
```python
class RiskState:
    timestamp: datetime
    open_positions: int
    daily_pnl: float
    current_drawdown: float
    exposure_by_symbol: Dict[str, float]
    exposure_by_sector: Dict[str, float]
    last_risk_check: datetime
    risk_score: float  # 0-1

class OrderState:
    order_id: str
    candidate_id: str
    symbol: str
    type: OrderType
    volume: float
    price: float
    sl: float
    tp: float
    status: OrderStatus
    filled_at: Optional[datetime]
    filled_price: Optional[float]
    slippage: Optional[float]
    commission: Optional[float]

class PositionState:
    position_id: str
    order_id: str
    symbol: str
    direction: TradeDirection
    volume: float
    entry_price: float
    current_price: float
    unrealized_pnl: float
    realized_pnl: float
    open_time: datetime
    last_update: datetime
```

### Core 5: Platform & Operations Core
**Responsibility:** Infrastructure, Monitoring, User Services

| Component | Function |
|-----------|----------|
| APIService | REST/WebSocket Gateway |
| MonitoringService | Health Checks, Metrics |
| AlertService | Notifications |
| SchedulerService | Cron Jobs |

### Core 6: Learning & Evolution Core
**Responsibility:** Experience Recording, Error Analysis, Model Updates

| Component | Function | Output |
|-----------|----------|--------|
| ExperienceLedger | Record Decisions | ExperienceRecord |
| ErrorClassifier | Categorize Failures | ErrorType |
| ReplayEngine | Re-decide Historical | Comparison |
| ModelRegistry | Version Control | ModelVersion |
| ChangeGovernor | Approve Updates | ChangeRequest |

**Experience Record Schema:**
```python
class ExperienceRecord:
    record_id: str
    decision_id: str
    timestamp: datetime
    market_snapshot: MarketSnapshot
    data_quality: DataQuality
    regime: RegimeSnapshot
    evidence: EvidenceSet
    thesis: Thesis
    candidate: TradeCandidate
    risk_decision: RiskDecision
    execution_plan: ExecutionPlan
    actual_result: ActualResult
    slippage: float
    fees: float
    delay_ms: float
    invalidation: Optional[InvalidationCondition]
    error_classification: Optional[ErrorType]
    lessons: List[str]

class ErrorType(Enum):
    EXPECTED_LOSS = "expected_loss"
    THESIS_ERROR = "thesis_error"
    DATA_ERROR = "data_error"
    EXECUTION_ERROR = "execution_error"
    RISK_ERROR = "risk_error"
    OPERATIONAL_ERROR = "operational_error"
    MODEL_DRIFT = "model_drift"
    UNKNOWN = "unknown"
```

### Core 7: AI Program Evolution (DSPy Compiler)
**Responsibility:** Evolve *how each school reasons and selects evidence*, never `what` executes.

DSPy (Stanford) is integrated as a **research-only `AI Program Compiler`** living
in a standalone package `ai/dspy/` (structure: `signatures/`, `modules/`,
`programs/`, `optimizers/`, `evaluators/`, `datasets/`, `registry/` +
`runtime.py` / `harness.py` / `worker.py` / `compiler.py` / `connect.py`).
It builds `Signatures → Modules → Evaluation → Optimization` pipelines over the
existing multi-school stack, improving analysis instructions/evidence selection
from validated outcomes — instead of hand-tuning prompts. Every program
consumes only **structured market state** (never raw OHLC) and outputs the
`SchoolEvidence` standard. Evaluation (`evaluators/metrics.py`, the
professional trading metric) must exist **before** any optimiser run.

| Component | Function | Authority |
|-----------|----------|-----------|
| SignatureContracts | Liquidity/Structure/Session/Fusion/Review contracts (`ai/dspy/signatures/contracts.py`) | Read-only |
| ProgramCatalogue + Programs | Catalogued evolvable programs (`ai/dspy/programs/`: `ict_scalping` = ICT/SMC module chain) over the module wrappers in `ai/dspy/modules/` | Produce structured verdicts |
| EvaluationMetric | Professional trading metric — decision/entry/risk quality, timing, MAE/MFE, R:R, regime, drawdown impact, false-positive penalty (`ai/dspy/evaluators/metrics.py`) + `evaluate_program` harness | Score candidates |
| ProgramCompiler | Version-safe optimiser wiring (GEPA → MIPROv2 → BootstrapFewShot, `ai/dspy/optimizers/`), run in a **watchdogged subprocess** (`ai/dspy/harness.py` → `worker.py`) | Produce candidate programs |
| ProgramRegistry | Per-version JSON promotion ladder (`research → candidate → validated → paper → production`/`quarantined`) (`ai/dspy/registry/programs.py`) | Propose promotion |
| CorpusAdapter | Strategy-lab observations as read-only training examples (`ai/dspy/datasets/strategy_lab.py`) | Read-only |
| ConnectHooks | Opt-in, behaviour-neutral hooks into `core/pipeline.py` + `operations/backtest.py` (`ai/dspy/connect.py`, env `TRADERAIG_DSPY_PIPELINE=1`) | Report only |
| StatusSurface | `/research/dspy/status` — gated, non-blocking, offline-safe | Report only |
| ModelRegistry | `/research/dspy/models` — LLM **adapter + model registry** (providers, probed local Ollama catalogue, configured model, env-only swap contract) | Report only |

**Governance contract (hard rules):**
- DSPy **never opens, sizes, vetos or routes a trade**. Quant/rule gates, Risk
  Governor and Execution are untouched by the layer.
- Activation is **explicit** (`TRADERAIG_DSPY_ENABLED=1`) and requires a
  configured LLM provider; importing `dspy` is lazy and may be network-bound,
  so the whole surface is safe in offline environments.
- All output feeds the **Strategy Lab validation gates** (`research →candidate
  → validated → paper → production → quarantined`); promotion stays in the
  registry governance path.

## [MILESTONES]

> **Status guide:** ⬜ pending | ✅ done | 🚧 in progress

### Phase 1: Control Plane (Weeks 1-4)
**Objective:** Establish identity, subscription, and audit infrastructure

| Milestone | Verifiable Goal | Status |
|-----------|-----------------|--------|
| M1.1 | User CRUD with authentication working | ✅ `governance/identity.py` |
| M1.2 | Subscription state machine (active/expired/suspended) | ✅ `grant_plan_entitlements`, `check_expirations` |
| M1.3 | Entitlement checks returning correct permissions | ✅ `can(user, entitlement)` |
| M1.4 | Audit log capturing all state changes | ✅ append-only `_audit` |
| M1.5 | PostgreSQL schema deployed and tested | ✅ SQLAlchemy models + SQLite dev / PG prod; **real PG16 deployment test proven 2026-09-25** (`scripts/postgres_prod_test.ps1`, full suite 584 passed on server) |

**Success Criteria:** ✅ users, subscriptions, entitlements, audit covered by unit tests.

| M7.8 | DSPy optimization harness (GEPA/MIPROv2/BootstrapFewShot) over replay + observation corpus feeding the validation lab (`ai/dspy/optimizers/run.py`; evaluation-before-optimization, registry candidate stamping) | ✅ scaffold **and execution proven with real learned state** — root-caused and fixed a demo-drop bug (`build_module` held the predictor in a closure ⇒ `named_predictors()` empty ⇒ DSPy silently discarded every bootstrapped demo); candidate `ict_scalping-v18` (`BootstrapFewShot` / `llama3.2:1b`) now persists **real few-shot demos** (`program_serialized: true`, artifact `registry/data/programs/ict_scalping-v18.json` 2.6 KB, compiled 0.4 vs baseline 0.384); compiled sessions ran on the local provider (candidates `v13..v18`); **model registry / adapter** added (`ai/dspy/models.py` + `GET /research/dspy/models`: adapters, probed Ollama catalogue, configured provider, env-only swap contract) so Llama/Qwen/Gemma/cloud can be switched without rebuilding school programs; lab intake remains a **read-only evidence dossier** (`ai/dspy/intake.py` + `proposals/*.json`); honest limit: sparse corpus gold ⇒ few-shot delta small until a stronger local LM or tradeable-adapter metric |

### Phase 8: Integration Quality Plane (Weeks 35-38)
**Objective:** Integrate Strategy Laboratory outputs into runtime decisioning with strict promotion, monitoring, and quality controls

| Milestone | Verifiable Goal | Status |
|-----------|-----------------|--------|
| M8.1 | Runtime consumes only validated strategy priors | ✅ `research/strategy_lab/priors.py` (`PriorProvider`, `PriorEdge`, `registry_entry_to_prior`, `default_provider`) — read-only projection of `registry.json`; hard eligibility rule (stage ∈ VALIDATED/PAPER/PRODUCTION **and** passing validation); wired into `core/pipeline.py` via `_resolve_prior` (live-only) at both candidate sites; safe-by-default when no eligible priors |
| M8.2 | Matrix/decision layer reads conditional edge by regime/session/asset | ✅ `PriorEdge.edge_for(regime, session)` (conditional buckets from `ValidationReport.regime_concentration`/`session_concentration`, `regimes_allowed`/`sessions_allowed` abstain gates); `PriorProvider.lookup`/`best_for` filters by asset/family/regime/session/min_edge; blended into `CandidateBoard._score_priority` (edge ±10 cap, +2 live-eligible); candidate provenance `strategy_id`/`strategy_stage`/`strategy_edge` |
| M8.3 | Risk Governor gates live usage by promotion stage | ✅ `RiskGovernor._check_strategy_gate` (`_PROMOTION_FLOOR="paper"`; authoritative `stage_resolver(candidate)` injection, fallback candidate stage; RESEARCH/CANDIDATE/VALIDATED/quarantined/unknown veto; no `strategy_id` ⇒ gate inactive) |
| M8.4 | Research learning separated from execution learning in reports and storage | ✅ `learning/learning_split.py` (`research_learning` off jsonl observation corpus vs `execution_learning` off filled Experience Ledger, separate `storage` labels, never merged) + `GET /learning/split` |
| M8.5 | Manual/system ownership remains isolated through research/runtime flows | ✅ `PerformanceAttributor.generate(ownership=...)`; `GET /performance` returns `{system, manual, note}` split and refuses to merge (default stays system-only) |

### Phase 9: Continuous Monitoring Plane (Weeks 39-42)
**Objective:** Operate the full stack with persistent monitoring, quality review, and continuous learning

| Milestone | Verifiable Goal | Status |
|-----------|-----------------|--------|
| M9.1 | Research run dashboard / summaries expose pass-fail bottlenecks | ✅ `GET /research/summary` (per-stage counts, validated count, `bottlenecks` = highest gate-fail first) + `GET /research/gates` (per-strategy gate table) |
| M9.2 | Promotion monitoring tracks `CANDIDATE`→`VALIDATED`→`PAPER`→`PRODUCTION` | ✅ `GET /research/promotions` — parses append-only registry history (`registered#stage`/`promote#stage`/`quarantine#reason`) into per-strategy timelines + current ladder counts |
| M9.3 | Runtime drift monitoring flags strategy degradation and quarantine transitions | ✅ `operations/drift.py` (`DriftMonitor`: anchored baseline, rolling window, win-rate/slippage/latency thresholds, consecutive-alert → quarantine *proposals* read-only) + `GET /monitoring/drift`; contract: drift only proposes, quarantine stays a governance act (`/research/quarantine`) |
| M9.4 | Macro freshness, ownership integrity, and confluence quality are monitored explicitly | ✅ `operations/quality.py` + `GET /monitoring/quality` — per-area verdicts (healthy/watch/degraded): macro freshness from `MacroContextUpdater.status()` vs a `2× refresh_seconds` stale limit; ownership integrity scans filled ledger records for unknown origin / missing `pnl` / implicit-origin (system/manual totals never merged, M8.5); confluence quality samples `SchoolBoard`'s new bounded deliberation history (multi_source ratio vs single-source-exception/blocked, confidence contract exposed) |
| M9.5 | Ongoing review loop updates strategy registry without destabilizing live execution | ✅ `ReviewLoop` in `operations/quality.py` + `GET /monitoring/review` (read-only queue: drift quarantine proposals + promotion candidates, each flagged `engine_running`) + `POST /ops/review/apply` — mutations deferred (409) while a LIVE/CEX engine is actively running the asset's loop (`default_engine_guard`); `_lab_lock` serializes writes; registry remains the sole promote/demote authority with audit history lines; guards are re-checked at application time, never trusting stale queue rows |

### Phase 10: Challenge Account Management Plane (Weeks 43-46)
**Objective:** Sell and enforce challenge-account protection as a paid service layer over the existing risk/governance/execution path — without becoming a second trading engine

Source: `new section9.md` §34 (service layer) and §35 (account experience & adaptive learning), integrated into `ENGINEERING MAST.md` as §34/§35.

| Milestone | Verifiable Goal | Status |
|-----------|-----------------|--------|
| M10.1 | A $9.99 product entitlement exists server-side, is independent of subscription plans, and is activated only by canonical billing | ✅ `governance/products.py` — `PRODUCTS["challenge_basic"]` ($9.99, 30d + 3d grace), `ProductState` ∈ {ACTIVE, GRACE_PERIOD, EXPIRED, CANCELLED, SUSPENDED}, `ProductService.grant/revoke/suspend/reinstate/has/status`, durable via `ProductEntitlementDB` + `ProductRepository`. `Entitlement.CHALLENGE_MANAGEMENT` added and **excluded from every `PLAN_ENTITLEMENTS`** so the plan answers what the platform may do and the product answers what was paid for (34.18) |
| M10.2 | Payment can buy the product, and only a verified webhook activates it in live mode | ✅ `governance/payments.py` — `ChargeResult.product_id`, `PaymentService.charge(product_id=…)`, `charge_product()`, `_activate_product()`; sandbox records SUCCEEDED directly, live charges stay `PENDING` until the provider-signed webhook flips them (34.2). API `GET /payments/products`, `POST /payments/product` |
| M10.3 | The entitlement gate resolves the *acting* caller, and the governor fails closed | ✅ `challenge/entitlement.py` — `acting_principal()` (request-scoped `current_caller()`, else `principal_user_id()`), `owns_challenge_management()`, `challenge_gate()` (bootstrap only when no principal exists at all). `risk/governor.py::_check_challenge_policy` refuses on the trade itself, which is where 34.15/34.18 actually bite; API routes allow bootstrap so a fresh install can still register a policy |
| M10.4 | Challenge API reads/registers state and never places an order | ✅ `challenge/live.py` is the single account/registry/headroom bridge (honours an injected `state_ref`, so an injected account is never answered from the process-wide state); `_check_challenge_policy` feeds `RiskGovernor`, which is the only order authority. Mutating routes are `_guard_challenge()`-gated; reads stay open by design |
| M10.5 | Position size is bounded by challenge headroom, not by the strategy alone | ✅ §34.9/§34.23 resize: `permission_for` emits `ALLOW_REDUCED_RISK` when a proposal overruns the buffered safe room but still fits the remaining room, and `PROTECT` only when *no* size fits; `compute_volume` caps `risk_capital` at `safe_room_usd()` (reusing the verdict `veto_reason()` already computed, keyed by candidate id). `_proposed_risk()` prices the proposal in cash using tick economics, never a raw price distance |
| M10.6 | The commercial layer has regression coverage at both the HTTP and the execution-gate level | ✅ `tests/test_operations/test_challenge_routes.py` (+6 commercial tests) and `tests/test_risk/test_challenge_execution_gate.py` (13 tests: unpaid, plan-only, bootstrap, policy veto, drawdown protect, unknown baseline, injected-state wiring, $700→resized, and the cache-key guard). Full suite **1147 passed** |

| M10.9 | Product lifecycle hooked to the billing failure path | ⬜ `ProductRepository.save()` merges on a random `entitlement_id`, so a renewal writes a second row per `(user_id, product_id)`; refunds do not revoke, and the webhook sets `product_activated=True` even when activation failed. §34.20 needs `CANCELLED` to be reachable from a failed charge, and `delete_for_user()` is not yet wired to identity deletion |

## [ORPHANS & PENDING]

### Recently Completed (was «Not Yet Started»)
- [x] **Wallet / payment service integration — sandbox-first (Stripe wired / Pi stub)**: `governance/payments.py` — per-user immutable wallet ledger + `PaymentProvider` abstraction (sandbox / Stripe / Pi) mirroring the CEX paper-gate discipline. **Sandbox** = deterministic in-process ledger, zero network, never locked; **Stripe** = real urllib REST, live only when `PAYMENTS_MODE=live` + `PAYMENTS_LIVE_ALLOWED=1` + key present (max 25,000 USD), live charges land `PENDING` and only a **verified webhook** (Stripe-style HMAC-SHA256 `t=…,v1=…` via `_verify_hs256`) flips to `SUCCEEDED` and activates the subscription; **Pi** = honest not-wired stub (never fakes success). Refund flips a succeeded charge to `REFUNDED` + mirrors the entry (ledger `refunded==2`, balance 0). API `GET /payments/status|providers`, `GET /payments/wallet/{id}`, `POST /payments/charge|refund|webhook` (404 unknown user, 400 bad plan/amount, 409 locked); console `tradraig pay status|providers|charge|wallet|refund` strictly via the API (single billing authority). Charging a plan activates the user's subscription (30d) through the identity layer. 16 new tests + live uvicorn proof 9/9 → **609 passed**
- [x] Telegram/Discord chained multi-channel routing: `operations/alerts.py` chains `telegram → discord → console` (`ALERT_CHANNELS`), fan-out (`channel="all"`) or failover (`channel="chain"`), console always the guaranteed last resort; Discord enabled by `DISCORD_WEBHOOK_URL` alone
- [x] **Redis pub/sub dashboard/copy-engine consumers**: `operations/event_feed.py` — transport-agnostic operations event bus (bounded in-process ring, resume-by-id `since(after)`, topic filter); every publish is mirrored to Redis via the existing `core.redis_state` (`traderaig:state:events`) when a server is available, otherwise the in-process feed carries the whole stream (zero-infra fallback). Publishers: `/ops/review/apply` (`transition`), change-board decisions (`requested`/`decided`). Consumers: `operations/copy_engine.py` — mirrors master transitions into a read-only follower view and flags governance disputes (promote→quarantine flips); API `GET /ops/events?after=&topic=` + `GET /ops/copy/follower`; console `tradraig events [--after] [--topic]` + `tradraig copy [--json]`; dashboard **Live Ops** page (event stream + follower view, live/mock). +13 tests (**545 passed**)
- [x] **Change Governance approval UI + Change Request tracker**: `governance/change_requests.py` — audited in-memory change board (`submit`/`approve`/`deny`/`cancel`, history + append-only audit via identity); approvals of `promote`/`quarantine` are applied through the same engine-safety guard as `/ops/review/apply` (engine-running → 409, request stays pending); API `GET/POST /governance/change-requests` + `/…/{id}/approve|deny|cancel`; console `tradraig change list|submit|approve|deny|cancel` (via the API = single governance authority); dashboard **Change Governance** page (`GovernancePage.tsx`, live-fallback mock) with approve/deny
- [x] **Account ↔ strategy bindings — the Research↔Account seam** (P1, resolved the corrected `ACCOUNT_REGISTRY_TRADE_SCHEMA_IMPACT.md` gap): `account_strategy_bindings` table (19th) + `AccountStrategyBindingRepository` + `accounts/strategy_bindings.py` domain (`bind`/`unbind`/`set_status`/`get`/`for_account`/`for_strategy`/`eligible_strategies`/`summary`). One research asset runs on N accounts without forking: keyed `(account_id, strategy_id, variant_hash)` where `strategy_id` carries the version and `variant_hash` proves researched provenance (defaulted to the registry's own digest on bind). Refuses unknown strategies, sub-`BINDABLE_STAGES` (`validated/paper/production`) stages, and variant mismatches; `pending` is never eligible; **no `grants_trade_permission` column and no authorisation method** — the Risk Governor stays the only trade authority (P0 fail-open lesson); `risk_override_json` nullable so "no override" ≠ "override 0". `AccountConsistencyChecker.orphaned_bindings()` flags bindings whose account vanished. 33 new tests (incl. enumerating the API for authorisation-like names — absence asserted, not docstringed); regression 102 passed (persistence + registry + trade schema); ruff clean; live smoke bound October Clash `5bd99e44…` → `btcusd:ict_liquidity_sweep_reversal:v7` (paper) against real `registry.json` (56 entries). Bindings are records, not execution: the one exposed surface is the read-only, ownership-guarded `GET /accounts/{account_id}/bindings` (`own_record_access()`, 403 no_principal / 404 unknown-account; 9 route tests in `tests/test_operations/test_account_binding_routes.py`, regression 185 passed). `guard_account()` wiring to routes (D4/D6) stays unwired while `TRADERAIG_AUTH=0` (D5); no write/execution surface exists.

### Open ORPHANS — TrederAIG 10-layer roadmap (from `D:\TraderAIG\New Expind dev.md`, adopted 2026-09-26; governed by ENGINEERING MAST §27/§28)

**Audit 2026-09-29 against the running system** (not against the document's own claims). The document says the core is already *"متكامل بدرجة كبيرة جدًا"* and names PostgreSQL/Redis/RabbitMQ; what is true on this host:
- **PostgreSQL — true but NOT the runtime.** A real PG16.15 deployment test is proven (584 passed on a real server) and the SQLAlchemy models are production-shaped, but `POSTGRES_HOST` is empty, so every live `/system/health` reads `SQLite persistence active`. SQLite is the deliberate dev/prod-host default, not a gap in the schema.
- **Redis — true and optional.** `core/redis_state.py` + `operations/event_feed.py` mirror every ops event to Redis when a server is reachable; `TRADERAIG_STATE_REDIS=0` keeps the whole stack on an in-process ring with zero infrastructure.
- **RabbitMQ — NOT implemented.** `New Expind dev.md` and the [TECH_STACK] table both list it; there is no `pika`/`amqp` dependency and no code path. Treat any "RabbitMQ Event System" claim as documentation drift, not a described feature. Corrected in [TECH_STACK].
- **Reconciliation `Degraded` is expected, not a defect.** `/system/health` reports `idle: matches internal state while no live venue is attached` until a real venue is attached. It is deliberately *not* green while there is no broker/exchange truth to reconcile against — an honest amber, not a failure to fix.

The honest answer to "is the integration finished": **the 10-layer core is integrated; the Product Scale layer is not.** These remain open, unchanged by the 2026-09-29 work:

- [ ] **Phase A — Identity/Entitlements (multi-tenant)**: Multi-tenant data + per-tenant isolation; Identity & Security Plane (account_ids, tenant scoping). Server-side authoritative entitlements itself is DONE — see the [x] line below. **Audited 2026-09-29: this is still 0% on tenancy.** `core.state` is one process-wide `CanonicalState` (account, positions, orders, risk) shared by every engine and only the `users` table carries an owner column, so there is no per-tenant state to isolate. What was delivered instead is the honest boundary: `_own()` stops a caller redirecting the principal's authority at another user's record, `/system/health.tenancy` states `single-tenant` / `authenticated: false` out loud, authority can no longer be removed by an empty request (`plan` is required, downgrades need `confirm_downgrade`, and `subscription/revoke` + `DELETE /users/{id}` are the sanctioned, audited ways to take authority away), and real isolation (per-user canonical state, ledgers, engines + an authenticated caller) remains this open item.
- [x] **Phase A — Entitlements enforcement (28.17)**: decision endpoints (risk policy, CEX/MT5 engine & execution, capital allocation, research promote/quarantine/revalidate, ops review apply, change requests, strategy intake test/promote) are server-side gated against the primary identity's subscription; the dashboard only reflects them. Bootstrap: no identity configured → gate reports inactive and passes (honest, not fake-open); once a primary identity exists the gate is hard (403 + audit on denial) until the subscription is activated.
- [x] **Phase A — Secrets Vault** (28.18 Security Boundary): `api_key`/`secret`/passphrase moved off the frontend and into a vault-backed store — DPAPI-encrypted at rest, env fallback for bootstrap, API returns set-bools only (never values), writes gated by API_ACCESS; CEX venues + Stripe/Pi billing read through the vault.
- [x] **Phase A — Portfolio Intelligence + Capital Allocation** (27.14 Institutional Risk Priority): portfolio-level risk aggregation, per-strategy capital allocation, cross-strategy correlation.
- [x] **Phase B — Opportunity Engine**: opportunity must pass the full institutional screen before it becomes a trade (27.10/28.11 Opportunity ≠ Trade); candidates surfaced to the operator with full evidence, not auto-executed. **Delivered** `decision/opportunity.py` (`Opportunity` + bounded/debounced durable `OpportunityBoard` + `OpportunityEngine.screen`), wired into both pipeline execute points (`core/pipeline.py`) before execution: every risk-approved setup is recorded with its full evidence dossier (governor checks, portfolio-allocation row, promotion stage, screen rubric) and risk-vetoed setups are recorded `REJECTED` with the real veto reason; `TRADERAIG_OPPORTUNITY_GATE=1` switches to operator confirmation (qualified opportunities never auto-execute — `POST /opportunities/{id}/approve` → executed by the pipeline on the next bar with a fresh execution-time Risk Governor check; `POST .../reject` closes it), default `0` keeps the governed auto-execution. API: `GET /opportunities/status`, `GET /opportunities`, `GET /opportunities/{id}` (full evidence), `GET /paper/requests` (dashboard gap closed from the board), console `tradraig opportunity status|list|show|approve|reject`. Approve gated by `CAN_TRADE`, reject by `CAN_MANAGE_RISK`, both audited.
- [x] **Phase B — Market Data Plane**: one canonical, provenance-tagged market read (28.6 Canonical Strategy Specification; §3.1 source-of-truth). `core/market_plane.py` — `MarketPlane` (thread-safe singleton) holds one bounded window per (symbol, timeframe) with **source precedence** `mt5 100 > cex 90 > bridge 50 > fallback 20 > backtest/demo 10`; a lower-ranked publish is recorded in `shadowed` and never mixed into an owner window. Quotes follow the same precedence, a `derived` (bar-close) quote can never displace a real tick, and the canonical quote object is mirrored into `core.state` so existing consumers keep working. Reads return `MarketRead` (bars + quote + source + version + real ages + `single_source` verdict); freshness is computed from real bar/tick clocks, never "file exists = healthy". Shared-pull throttle (`min_pull_interval`) so N consumers cause one terminal round-trip, plus `max_age_seconds` for polling producers. Wired: `execution/mt5_adapter.py` (`sync_market`/`sync_candles` + `mt5` feed), `operations/live_engine.py` and `operations/cex_engine.py` (`cex:<venue>` + real public book-ticker), `operations/api.py` (`/market/data|/market/quote|/market/feeds`, chart route, and `/system/health.market_data` now reporting real feed freshness instead of corpus size). **Terminal market channel (prices only flow terminal → server)**: `execution/bridge.py` gains a separate `market/<SYMBOL>.json` file transport (atomic writes, the signal file stays the single trade authority), `operations/ws_bridge.py` accepts a token-gated, rate-limited client `market` frame ingested as `bridge:<session>`, `operations/market_bridge.py` polls the file path into the plane (`TRADERAIG_MARKET_BRIDGE=1`, off by default; `GET /bridge/market` reports real ingest counters and every rejection), `scripts/mt5_bridge_proxy.py --market` forwards changed frames, and `mt5/TraderAIG.mq5` v1.100 publishes its own real tick + forming bar (never compiled/run here — see Blocked). Live proof 2026-09-28 on a real uvicorn boot: WS market frame and EA-shaped frame file both reached the plane with their real source, a junk frame was rejected with its reason, and that pass fixed three honesty defects (health named the pull registry instead of the publisher; the poller never started under uvicorn operations.api:app; unchanged frames inflated the ingest counters) - all three now have regression tests. Frames missing a symbol, an unknown timeframe, a junk value, a one-sided quote or a real tick time are **rejected with the reason, never defaulted**. **Strategy builder (28.6)**: `research/strategy_lab/market_spec.py` derives `required market data` / `required features` from the live `FamilySpec` the scorer actually uses, detects the §28.6 ambiguous expressions ("strong move", "near support", …) and names the measured definition used, and `market_provenance()` reports which source/age/freshness produced a run (synthetic is explicitly `live=False`); vet results and run results now carry these blocks plus explicit `assumptions` and a thin-series warning. Tests: **879 passed** (`tests/test_core/test_market_plane.py` 95, bridge market channel + file transport in `tests/test_operations/test_ws_bridge.py`, `tests/test_strategy_lab/test_market_spec.py` 18). MQL5/MetaEditor compilation and live attach are NOT verified here.

## [LATEST_INTEGRATED_STATUS]

### Verified Current State
- Full Python verification baseline raised to **`369 passed`** (latest full suite, 143.7s) covering governance, persistence, ownership separation, macro runtime wiring, Pattern Geometry, SchoolBoard confluence, Strategy Lab, the live chart/context surface, and the DSPy AI-program layer (`ai/dspy/`).
- Governance is now restart-safer via persistence-backed rehydration and DB-first audit read fallback.
- MT5-originated records are classified into **`system`** vs **`manual`** ownership; manual trades are excluded from system performance attribution by default.
- `MacroSchool` is now minimally wired into runtime through `operations/macro_feed.py`, with freshness and status exposed through `LiveEngine`.
- `PatternGeometrySchool` is integrated as a **confirmation-first** layer inside the SchoolBoard and currently supports: `flag`, `triangle`, `double top/bottom`, `wedge`, and `rectangle` patterns.
- SchoolBoard deliberations now expose **`multi_source`** vs **`single_source_exception`** and record supporting sources/anchors for auditability.
- **DSPy (Stanford) AI-program compiler layer** is integrated at `ai/dspy/` (moved out of `research/` into the standalone `ai/` namespace): program signature contracts (liquidity / structure / session / confluence / review), module wrappers + `ict_scalping` program (ICT/SMC module chain), a **professional trading metric** (`evaluators/metrics.py` — decision/entry/risk quality, timing, MAE/MFE, R:R, regime, drawdown impact, false-positive penalty) plus a dspy-free `evaluate_program` harness, a per-version **promotion registry** (JSON, research→…→production), a strategy-lab corpus adapter, a **lazy, gated runtime** that never imports `dspy` in-process (import is network-dependent and may block offline), a non-blocking `/research/dspy/status` surface, and **behaviour-neutral opt-in hooks** in `core/pipeline.py` (evidence refinement, `TRADERAIG_DSPY_PIPELINE=1`) and `operations/backtest.py` (metric-bound post-run recap). DSPy is confined to *research/evolution* — it never touches execution routing, sizing, veto, or orders. See `ai/dspy/README.md`.
- Operational proof succeeded: proof-only workflow, live MT5 probe, and paper watch all completed successfully on the current environment.

### Highest-Efficiency Integrated Next Step
- The **Strategy Laboratory** subsystem (`research/strategy_lab/`) is now functional: contracts/observations, replay+features, genome runner, validation lab, and promotion registry (M7.2–M7.6) **plus** the DSPy compiler layer (M7.7).
- Next step (**M7.8**): run the **DSPy optimization harness** (MIPROv2 → BootstrapFewShot) over the replay + observation corpus in a networked LLM environment, and have its candidate programs flow into the existing validation lab gates.
- ✅ M7.8 **compile-stage executed on this machine** via the local provider (`E:\MLL` Ollama; `llama3.2:1b` + `llama3.2:3b` in `E:\MLL\models`): BootstrapFewShot compiles completed (candidates `ict_scalping-v13..v18`). A root-cause fix made serialization real: the STL `module` wrapper kept the predictor in a closure, so DSPy's `named_predictors()` was empty and every bootstrapped demo was silently dropped (`predictor2name[id] → KeyError → continue`); with the predictor bound as an instance attribute, candidate **v18** persisted **real demos** (`program_serialized: true`, 2.6 KB artifact, compiled 0.4 vs baseline 0.384). Learned-delta is still small because the corpus gold is sparse — stronger local model or a tradeable-adapter metric is the next lever. A **model registry / LLM adapter** (`ai/dspy/models.py` + `GET /research/dspy/models`) makes providers swappable via env alone (local/openai/anthropic), per the `LLM Adapter → DSPy → School Program → Evaluator → Model Registry` contract. Larger/stronger-model sweeps remain.
- Parallel follow-up (**Phase 8/9**): runtime-consuming-only-validated priors, promotion monitoring, and research-run dashboards.
- Do **not** overload `operations/backtest.py` with experiment-grid logic; DSPy output must feed the lab gates as read-only evidence change proposals.

### Engineering Master Alignment
This project should continue following the `ENGINEERING MAST.md` execution order:
1. inspect before modifying,
2. plan before implementing,
3. prefer minimal safe changes,
4. preserve existing architecture unless change is explicit,
5. never claim success without verification.

Applied guidance for the next subsystem:
- Strategy Lab must be built as an isolated module under `research/`, not fused into live execution paths.
- Every new phase must ship with targeted tests before widening integration.
- Contracts and schemas come before runners and orchestration.
- Validation gates must be explicit and reproducible before any production promotion path is opened.

### Integrated Quality Direction
To maximize system quality as one coherent operating core, future work should remain coupled through contracts, not ad-hoc logic:
- **Schools** generate setups and features.
- **Pattern Geometry** enriches confirmation quality.
- **Macro** supplies regime veto/context.
- **Quant** enforces statistical quality and sizing discipline.
- **Strategy Lab** measures conditional edge and promotion fitness.
- **Decision/Matrix** consumes validated priors.
- **Risk Governor** remains the hard operational gate.
- **Execution** stays isolated from research experimentation.
- **Learning** remains split between execution learning and research learning.
- **Monitoring** must cover research runs, promotion states, and runtime/live drift separately.

### Phase Execution Standard (Engineering Master Aligned)
Every upcoming phase should be executed using the following disciplined order:
1. **Repository discovery** of only the relevant files and interfaces.
2. **Source-of-truth check** against user requirements, `PROJECT_MAP.md`, `ENGINEERING MAST.md`, tests, and current implementation.
3. **Requirement and impact analysis** before code changes.
4. **Minimal reversible implementation** with architecture-preserving boundaries.
5. **Targeted tests** for the changed surface before broad integration.
6. **Validation run** on the larger suite before the phase is considered complete.
7. **Documentation update** in `PROJECT_MAP.md`, specs, and decision logs.

### Phase-Level Quality Gates
- No phase is complete without **verifiable artifacts**: code, tests, docs, and a clear status update.
- No runtime integration phase should begin before the prior research/contract phase has passing targeted tests.
- No production-promotion path should be opened before validation, ownership integrity, and monitoring are all explicitly covered.
- Any subsystem that can veto, route, size, or promote strategies must expose audit-friendly metadata.

## [DECISION_LOG]

| Date | Decision | Rationale | alternatives |
|------|----------|-----------|--------------|

| 2026-09-29 | **A publishing producer can no longer starve a higher-ranked feed - the last open market-plane defect, found live**: the canonical M15 window was owned by `bridge:file` with 6 bars, `pulls: 0` and `single_source: false`, while `/system/health.market_data` said `Healthy` and `M1/M5/H1` were all clean `mt5`. | 733 | 2026-09-29 | **The session had to become the authority, or authentication was decoration: a second user with a password could act as the operator**: row 732 shipped a session layer and, in the same breath, left every authority check still comparing against `identity.principal_user_id()`. Everything that decided what they could do still ran against the operator, and the proof was immediate rather than theoretical: logged in as user B, `GET /users/{operator}` returned **200** through the very `_own` guard that was added in row 730 to stop exactly that read, and `POST /users/{operator}/subscription/activate` with `plan:"free"` was stopped only by the confirm-downgrade guard from row 731, not by identity. Authentication that changes nothing is the same failure as the fabricated login screen one layer down, and the row-732 verification would not have caught it because every test logged in as the principal. **The session now determines the caller.** A request-scoped `contextvars.ContextVar` carries the authenticated user, and `_own` / `_guard` / the audit actor all resolve through `_acting_actor()`. A contextvar rather than a parameter threaded through every route: with **no session presented it resolves to `None` and every existing path behaves exactly as before**, which is the property that matters most here — the 24/7 host runs with enforcement off, and a security change must not be able to take the platform down at the worst possible moment. The machine service token deliberately still acts **as the operator**: the watchdog maintains the operator's own rails, and treating the machine credential as a lesser caller would lock the supervisor out of the system it maintains — the platform-disables-itself shape this project keeps having to undo. Two consequences worth stating: the entitlement gate now evaluates the *session holder* (a `free` user cannot `can_trade` merely for knowing a password), and **deleting a user now revokes their live session** — the token is checked against the user store on every use, because a token is 12h of HMAC and a user id, and `DELETE /users/{id}` that leaves a working credential behind would be deletion wearing revocation's name. **A test that lied about what it checked.** The contextvar is reset in a `finally`, and the first test written for it asserted "one caller must not leak into the next request". Sabotaging the reset left the suite **fully green** — Starlette runs each request in its own task with a copied context, so the reset is defensive rather than load-bearing, and the HTTP-level test could not see it either way. Rather than keep a test whose docstring claimed a guarantee it never checked (the exact habit rows 730-732 exist to remove), the HTTP test was rewritten to assert only what it can observe, and the binding is now unit-tested directly where removing the reset does fail. 8 tests (1029 -> 1035); 4 new sabotages confirmed red, including the two the first version of these tests had missed. Gate 6/6, 1035 passed. **Rejected**: refusing any session that is not the principal (rejected: it makes a second credential a credential with no power while pretending to be authorization, and it would break the moment a second operator is real); passing the caller as a parameter to every guarded route (rejected as the same 1029-test blast radius for no additional safety — the contextvar is request-scoped, so the two are equivalent in behaviour and very different in risk); letting a deleted user's token run until its 12h expiry (rejected: "the user is gone" must not be a state a credential can still act in, and an incident response that deletes a user should not leave them logged in); relying on the framework's per-request context isolation and dropping the reset (rejected: that makes a security control's correctness a property of a dependency's internals rather than of this code) | A control that authenticates a caller and then ignores that answer is worse than no control, because it is a control that has been *seen* to work. The question worth asking is not "is the token valid" but "what does validity change" — and the honest answer for the whole milestone was: nothing | Treating the operator as the acting identity because the system is single-tenant (rejected: single-tenant describes the shared *state*, not the identity of the caller, and conflating them is precisely what let a second user borrow the operator's authority); keeping the first leak test as written because it passed (rejected: a green test that cannot fail on the defect it names is worse than no test, because it is counted as coverage) |
| 732 | 2026-09-29 | **Authentication (28.19): a door that is either real or visibly absent — enforcement stays OFF by default, and the system says so in `/system/health`**: row 730 closed the side door (a caller could not redirect the operator's authority at another user's record) and stated plainly what remained: `_guard` evaluates entitlements against the principal the *server* names, and nobody is ever identified, so every caller acts with the operator's authority. This milestone adds the missing identification layer, and its defining constraint was not security theatre but **honesty about what is enforced**. The tempting version of this work is a login form on the dashboard and JWTs in the backend — the exact fabrication row 730 deleted, rebuilt one layer up, because a token that is never verified and a password that is never checked authenticate nobody while looking like they do. So enforcement is **opt-in via `TRADERAIG_AUTH=1`, default off**, and when off the system advertises that fact: `/system/health.auth` reports `enforcement_enabled: false` and the tenancy block now reads `authenticated: false` with the words "NOT enforced" (before, `authenticated` was hard-coded `false` while the guard was in fact trusting every caller — a field that was accidentally true for the wrong reason is worse than a field that is never written). **Reads stay open by design and are labelled as such.** Gating the 64 global read routes would produce a UI implying isolation that does not exist: `core.state` is one shared `CanonicalState`, so an authenticated reader reads the operator's account, positions and ledger anyway. Filtering per-caller without per-user state is a lie about isolation, so the control applies to mutation only and `reads_gated: false` is reported as a first-class field. **Enabling it is a one-way door, and the runbook says so.** Enforcement on with no credential set is a lockout: nobody can log in, therefore nobody can set a password. That is a real operational hazard rather than an edge case, so the route returns 401 with an explicit reason, the recovery path (the machine token) is implemented **and tested**, and the health block carries `service_token_configured` so a 24/7 host cannot silently strand its own watchdog — the failure mode this project keeps meeting, where the platform takes itself offline. The `TAIG-Backend-Watchdog`, the console and the smoke script are machine callers: they send `X-Service-Token` and the policy exempts `/payments/webhook` (provider-signed, never has a session) and the docs. **The default stays off deliberately**: a change that breaks the supervisor on an unattended box is worse than a documented open door, and the previous milestone's own lesson was that a written warning is not a control — this one ships a flag, a status field, a health assertion and a recovery procedure. `/auth/logout` is the one auth route that **requires** a session and is deliberately excluded from the open list: an earlier version excluded the whole `/auth/` prefix for convenience and logout then had no way to know which session to end. **Two defects were found by the tests rather than by reading the code.** (1) The middleware only evaluated a token when enforcement was ON, so with the door unlocked `/auth/session` told someone holding a *valid* session that they were unauthenticated — the dashboard would have been told a lie about its own state; requiring a session and *recognising* one are different questions and only the first is conditional. (2) `test_every_mutating_route_is_covered_by_the_policy` walks the real route table, but it asserts that paths **in** the open list are treated as open — so *adding* `/auth/logout` to that list kept the whole suite green. A policy that is only asserted against itself is a comment, not a control, so the open list is now pinned to its exact justified contents and **Live reloaded and verified**: the old backend (pid 7432) was stopped and `ensure_backend.py --start-mt5-sync --start-paper-sync --start-cex-sync` brought the new code up as pid 8880 with all three rails OK; an immediate rerun returned exit 0 (already running, nothing to do). The live SQLite store took the two additive `users` columns and still holds 6 users, `/auth/session` reports no session, a bad login is 401, feeds are `cex:binance mt5`, dashboard :8443 is HTTP 200, and `overall` is Degraded on `reconciliation` alone (no live venue attached), which is the documented expectation. Enforcement remains OFF on the live host, so no 24/7 rail changed behaviourally. 13 sabotage mutations (enforcement off, reads gated, logout opened, webhook opened, signature check skipped, expiry skipped, revocation skipped, hash reduced to the plaintext, health emptied, tenancy block flipped to a lie, …) were each confirmed to turn the suite red. 25 tests, full gate green, **1029 passed** (1002 -> 1029). **Rejected**: enabling enforcement by default (rejected: it would 403 the platform's own watchdog on an unattended 24/7 host the first night, and a security default that takes the service down is a denial-of-service written by the author); gating reads as well (rejected: false comfort — see above, the shared `CanonicalState` means a per-caller read filter hides nothing while implying isolation that is not there); a third-party JWT/auth dependency (rejected: stdlib `scrypt` + HMAC + a self-describing digest solves it with no new dependency, and the project rule is stdlib-first — a bare hash column also silently becomes wrong the day the cost parameters change, which is why the digest carries its own `$`-separated parameters); durable logout via DB-backed revocation (rejected as out of scope and stated as a limit — the denylist is in memory, so a backend restart revives outstanding sessions; bounded by the 12h TTL, and the runbook says so rather than implying a guarantee); letting the same credential be replaced with `current_password` supplied only by the service token (rejected: the service token is for machine callers, and letting it silently rewrite a human's password is exactly the kind of authority that should need a named path) | A control the operator cannot see is indistinguishable from a control that does not exist, so the milestone had to deliver two things together: a session layer that is genuinely verified, and a truthful statement of when it is switched on. The hard part was resisting the shape that looks like progress — a login page and tokens that nothing checks — because that is the same lie row 730 found, and a 24/7 host makes it worse: a default-on door that locks out the supervisor is a platform that disables itself | Pinning the open list exactly (accepted over a looser assertion: the route-walking test could not catch a path being *added* to the open list, which the sabotage run proved, so the set is asserted literally and a future addition has to justify itself in review); leaving `logout` on the open list because revoking is harmless (rejected: an endpoint that cannot identify its own session cannot do its only job); reporting auth status in the dashboard before the backend could enforce it (rejected: the surface would have shown a login for a door that does not exist — the DashboardFake failure mode, and the reason the backend flag is the thing that ships first) |
| 731 | 2026-09-29 | **A user_id is not an authority (Phase A, part 2): stop an empty request from deleting the operator's permissions**: the previous milestone made the entitlement gate solid, and the very next honest question was what the gate was actually *standing on*. Two things turned out to be missing underneath it. **First, the "activate" endpoint was also the "deactivate" endpoint, and one empty body reached it.** `POST /users/{id}/subscription/activate` read `payload.get("plan", "free")`, so `{}` meant "downgrade to free"; `grant_plan_entitlements()` replaces the whole entitlement set, so one unlabelled POST stripped `can_trade` from the operating principal — and since `/mt5/connect` and every gated decision route are guarded by that entitlement, the platform's own `TAIG-Backend-Watchdog` starts getting 403s on its next tick. **The system took itself offline in response to a malformed request.** That is the same silent-downgrade shape as the earlier "pay a smaller amount" incident arriving through a different door, which is the tell that it was a design flaw rather than a bug: granting authority and removing it were the same unlabelled operation. `plan` is now required and named (400 otherwise, and the message says why the old default was dangerous); a plan that would *remove* effective entitlements returns 409 listing, by name, exactly which entitlements are being given up, and needs `confirm_downgrade: true`. The guard asks what is **effectively** held rather than what is stored, so a pending/expired subscription is not treated as holding a `can_trade` it never had — otherwise a fresh install could never be enrolled, which is the deadlock the original code was defending against. **Second, there was no way to remove authority at all**, and this milestone had just documented that as a governance defect: revoking meant editing the database by hand or abusing the downgrade path. `POST /users/{id}/subscription/revoke` is the sanctioned verb, audited under its own action with the revoked entitlement list recorded, and `DELETE /users/{id}` actually removes the record — audited, durable, and ordered: a user with a live subscription must be revoked first (409 pointing at the revoke route), because deleting an authorised record outright is the same silent removal described less clearly, and deleting the acting principal needs `confirm=true` because it changes *who* the principal is, not merely that a user is gone. `confirm_lockout` is required to revoke yourself, and that guard refuses to block the act rather than pretend to protect: sometimes "stop the system trading" is exactly the intent, so the requirement is to say so, not to be stopped by a safety check that has no authority to stop an operator. `UserRepository.delete` was **missing entirely**, so a delete would have cleared memory while leaving the row in SQLite — the user would have been rehydrated on the next boot and the operator had no reason to doubt it had worked; the new test exercises the repository through `bind_test_session()` because the live environment has persistence on and the test environment has it off, so neither one alone covers the guarantee. **A second defect surfaced while fixing the first, and it is the more embarrassing one: the test suite was green only by accident of collection order.** The new autouse fixture in the payment tests deleted `IDENTITY_PRIMARY_EMAIL` but did not restore the identity store, and with no primary email set `principal_user_id()` falls back to the first-created user — so a throwaway payment user, left on a `starter` plan, became the acting principal for whatever ran next, and 28 gated tests failed on `can_manage_risk`. It was invisible in a full-suite run **only because alphabetical collection happens to run `test_operations` before `test_payments`**; collected the other way round the suite was red. A green suite that is a coin-flip on ordering is not green, so the fixture now snapshots and restores the user maps, and both orders are asserted green. 18 tests (984 -> 1002), and each of the five new guards was individually sabotaged to confirm it actually fails. Live re-check against the running backend: `{}` -> 400, unconfirmed `free` -> 409 naming the five lost entitlements, self-revoke -> 409 naming the watchdog consequence, delete-with-active-subscription -> 409, and the operator's `institutional`/`can_trade` were unchanged after all four attempts, with paper and CEX rails still running. **Rejected**: hard-blocking self-revocation as "protection" (rejected: a guard that blocks a legitimate operator decision in order to feel safe is theatre — the honest shape is a required confirmation that states the consequence); keeping the `plan` default but validating that `{}` means free (rejected: that is the bug. The default itself is the defect, because a caller who expresses no intent must never be the reason authority disappears); allowing delete of an active user (rejected: it would reintroduce the silent removal the same PR closes, merely with a different verb); treating "no user revocation route" as a documentation-only gap (rejected: it is a live hole — revoking access by hand is exactly the kind of out-of-band mutation the single-authority convention exists to prevent) | An entitlement gate is only as real as the floor beneath it, and the floor was "send `{}` and the operator stops being able to trade". Six routes accepted a caller-supplied `user_id` — `GET /users/{id}`, `GET /users/{id}/can/{ent}`, `GET|PUT /users/{id}/risk-policy`, `POST /users/{id}/subscription/activate`, `GET /payments/wallet/{id}` — and none of them asked who was asking. **`POST /payments/charge` was the worst of them and the path-parameter sweep missed it because the id arrives in the BODY**: it activates a real subscription, and it had been left deliberately ungated because gating activation was feared to deadlock a new principal into never acquiring entitlements. Ownership stops a caller redirecting that authority at another user's record; it does not establish who the caller is. That needs an authentication layer, which is a separate milestone. The exploit-verification user created in the live store was removed out-of-band (the audit rows recording it are deliberately preserved — an append-only trail is the last thing to edit during an incident) which itself exposed a governance gap: **there is no user-deletion or entitlement-revocation route**, so revoking access here means editing the database by hand or using the downgrade footgun. 24 new tests (960 -> 984), including source-fact guards that fail if a hard-coded operator identity or fabricated metric returns, and a guard that reads the route decorators and fails if any `user_id` route lacks a `_own(` call; every new guard was confirmed to go red when its clause is removed, and an earlier loose regex (`98.3`) that also matched inside the real price `"$198.30"` was tightened because a guard that fires on innocent data teaches people to ignore it. Two of those states were unreachable bugs on their own: `challenge_gate().get("active")` crashed when no entitlement existed (a bootstrap install 500'd instead of trading), and the live bridge read the process-wide `CanonicalState` instead of the `state_ref` it was handed — an injected account was graded against whatever the API process happened to have. (2) **`ALLOW_REDUCED_RISK` was dead code, and §34.8 vs §34.23 was a genuine contradiction** — surfaced and ruled by the user rather than picked silently. §34.8 says "a strategy signal cannot override insufficient account headroom"; §34.23's worked example says "Strategy Risk = $700 → Challenge allows only $420 → **Resize to ≤ $420** → Governance". Repo-shipped test `test_open_risk_that_exceeds_the_remaining_room_protects_rather_than_allows` (commit `50cd430`) had taken the first reading alone and asserted PROTECT for 1,900 against a 1,800 buffered / 2,000 gross room — structurally the same case. **Ruling: resize, and PROTECT only when no size fits.** The rung now fires when the proposal overruns the safe room but fits the remaining room, and that test was rewritten to pin all three boundaries (1,500 allow / 1,900 resize to 1,800 / 2,500 protect). (3) **The resize then turned out to be inert**, and the failure was a shape mismatch rather than logic: `safe_room_usd` read `report["daily_loss"]` while `headroom_report` nests its figures under `report["dimensions"]`, so it found nothing and returned `None` — the cap silently not applying while every trade looked correctly sized. Proven end-to-end now: 0.7 lots × $1,000/lot = $700 proposed against a $750 room with a 10% buffer → `ALLOW_REDUCED_RISK` → computed `risk=$675.00`, `volume 0.67` at the broker's 0.01 step. (4) **Risk was priced in the wrong unit.** `_proposed_risk` multiplied size by `|entry − stop|`, which on XAUUSD (tick 0.01, tick value $1) turned a $5,000 trade into $5 — a position carrying 3× the remaining headroom reported itself as having room.
