---

## [CONVENTIONS]

### Code Style
- Python: PEP 8, type hints required
- MQL5: Standard MT5 conventions
- Naming: snake_case for Python, camelCase for MQL5

### Testing
- Unit tests: pytest (Python), manual (MQL5)
- Integration tests: End-to-end flow validation
- Replay tests: Historical decision reproduction

### Documentation
- All public APIs documented
- Architecture decisions recorded in DECISION_LOG
- State schemas maintained in this file

---

## [QUICK_COMMANDS]

```bash
# Development
python -m pytest tests/           # Run Python tests
docker-compose up -d              # Start services
docker-compose logs -f api        # View API logs

# 24/7 trading host (this Windows box until a VPS exists)
python scripts/mt5_host_watchdog.py                # data-freshness check, report-only, nothing restarted
python scripts/mt5_host_watchdog.py --restart      # opt-in relaunch, rate limited (3/hour + 120s cooldown)
python scripts/mt5_host_watchdog.py --ensure-terminal   # start the terminal if down (boot path)
python scripts/ensure_backend.py --start-mt5-sync # ensure :8000 healthy + re-assert the tier-100 mt5 rail
Get-Content logs\watchdog.log -Tail 20            # what the 5-minute terminal task reported
Get-Content logs\backend_boot.log -Tail 10        # what the 5-minute backend task reported

# Operator console (bilingual EN/AR, stdlib-only, works with or without the API up)
python tools/tradraig.py status                # per-layer IIOS snapshot (RAM-aware)
python tools/tradraig.py --lang ar status      # نفس الشاشة بالعربية
python tools/tradraig.py doctor                # self-diagnosis + fixes (--json for machine output; exit 1 on hard fail)
python tools/tradraig.py start all             # backend + dashboard + ollama, detached, logs→logs/
python tools/tradraig.py stop backend          # stop any layer by port
python tools/tradraig.py gate on|off           # flip machine-level DSPy gate (User-scope, persisted)
python tools/tradraig.py board                 # probe every dashboard feed: live/empty/static verdict (--json)
python tools/tradraig.py user create --email exec@inst.com --plan institutional   # identity (via API/authority)
python tools/tradraig.py user list             # list identities
python tools/tradraig.py exercise [--seconds 20] [--status]   # in-process market exercise (fills live veto/learning feeds)
python tools/tradraig.py info                  # versions/models/RAM
python tools/tradraig.py events [--after N] [--topic registry|change|risk|health|ops]   # tail the operations event feed (live API)
python tools/tradraig.py copy [--json]      # copy-engine follower mirror (master-decision view, live API)
python tools/tradraig.py change list|submit [--kind promote|quarantine|config --target T --reason R]|approve ID|deny ID|cancel ID  # audited change-request board (via API)
python tools/tradraig.py pay status|providers|charge --user UID --amount 49 --plan starter|wallet UID|refund --charge ID   # billing/wallet (sandbox-first; via API = single billing authority)
python tools/tradraig.py strategy propose --asset XAUUSD --session london --min-rr 2.5 --size-r 1.0 --family fvg-continuation --notes "..."|vet --id ID|test --id ID|list [--status VETTED]|show --id ID|promote --id ID [--reason]   # user strategy intake: describe -> project vets -> lab tests -> governed promotion (via API)
python scripts/cex_okx_probe.py        # read-only signed OKX balance probe (live slot, IPv4 whitelisted) — exit 0 = OK
python scripts/cex_okx_demo_probe.py   # read-only signed OKX balance probe (demo/testnet slot, x-simulated-trading:1) — exit 0 = OK
python -m ruff check .                              # lint (must be 0)
powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File scripts\verify_all.ps1  # whole verification gate in one command (ruff→py_compile→pytest→tsc/vite→smoke)
powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File scripts/smoke_live.ps1   # boot the real server + drive the console over HTTP (7-step live wiring check)
powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File scripts\postgres_prod_test.ps1 -Port 55432 -DataDir "E:\Postgres\data16"  # real PostgreSQL deployment test (WMI-launched server; full suite 584 passed)
python scripts/mt5_bridge_proxy.py --url ws://127.0.0.1:8000/bridge/ws --bridge-dir "%MT5_COMMON_DATA%" --once   # terminal-side real-time proxy: signals down, acks/status up (needs websockets)
python scripts/mt5_bridge_live_proof.py      # one-command real-time MT5 bridge certification: boots the real API (in-process uvicorn), runs the real proxy, publishes one APPROVED signal, asserts file-bridge relay + upstream ack — exit 0 = PASS; --port N to pin the port; --go-live to write into MT5_COMMON_DATA instead of a scratch dir
curl http://127.0.0.1:8000/system/health        # aggregated UPDATE.md §50 component health

# AI Program Compiler (DSPy; optional, research/evolution layer)
pip install -r requirements-ai.txt                # dspy extra
curl http://127.0.0.1:8000/research/dspy/status     # gated status (safe offline)
curl http://127.0.0.1:8000/research/dspy/models      # LLM adapter + model registry (providers, local catalogue, configured)
curl http://127.0.0.1:8000/research/dspy/registry      # read-only candidate ladder (provenance)
python tools/tradraig.py dspy status                    # local snapshot (never imports dspy; registry count fallback)
python tools/tradraig.py dspy optimize --model llama3.2:1b --trials 6   # dispatch a compile in a watchdogged worker
python tools/tradraig.py dspy proposal                  # refresh the latest candidate dossier → proposals/*.json
# enable: set TRADERAIG_DSPY_ENABLED=1 + OPENAI_API_KEY (or ANTHROPIC_API_KEY) in a
# networked environment, then drive optimize() via ai.dspy.compiler
# local provider (6GB box): TRADERAIG_DSPY_LM_BASE_URL + TRADERAIG_DSPY_LM + TRADERAIG_DSPY_LM_API_KEY
# NOW LIVE ON THIS MACHINE → E:\MLL\Ollama\ollama.exe serve (models E:\MLL\models):
#   TRADERAIG_DSPY_LM_BASE_URL=http://127.0.0.1:11434/v1
#   TRADERAIG_DSPY_LM=llama3.2:1b
#   TRADERAIG_DSPY_LM_API_KEY=ollama
#   (provider set User-scope; /research/dspy/status → lm shows local llama3.2:1b ready)
#   /research/dspy/models → adapters (local/openai/anthropic) + probed catalogue llama3.2:3b,llama3.2:1b
# Real compile proven here: compiler.optimize → BootstrapFewShot, candidates v13..v18;
#   v18 program_serialized=true (real few-shot demos, artifact 2.6KB), compiled 0.4 vs baseline 0.384
# optional live hooking: set TRADERAIG_DSPY_PIPELINE=1 (evidence refinement +
# backtest metric recap). Structure/usage: see ai/dspy/README.md
python -m ai.dspy.worker optimize --programs ict_scalping --trials 6 --sleep 0   # driver

# Database
alembic upgrade head              # Run migrations
alembic downgrade -1              # Rollback migration

# MT5
# Compile EA in MetaEditor
# Deploy to MT5 Data Folder
```

| 734 | 2026-09-29 | **A logout that only survives until the next restart is not a logout: revocation had to become durable, and the field that reports it had to stop crying wolf**: row 732 shipped the denylist in memory and **documented the limit honestly** rather than hiding it — bounded by the 12h TTL, and the runbook said a backend restart revives outstanding sessions. That was a true statement about a weak guarantee, which is the most this project can ask of a limit, but the limit itself was the defect: a credential revoked because a person used a shared machine stayed valid through any watchdog restart, and on a 24/7 host the watchdog restarts are routine rather than exceptional. Revocations are now persisted to `TRADERAIG_REVOCATION_FILE` (default `.data/session_revocations.json`) holding **only `jti` and expiry** — no secret, no whole token — written atomically via `tmp` + `os.replace` so a crash mid-write cannot leave a half-written denylist that silently reads as "nothing revoked". Loads at boot and prunes expired entries, which is what stops the file becoming an append-only ledger of every session the system ever issued; pruning **on load** rather than on write is deliberate, because a host that boots more often than it logs out would otherwise accumulate entries for tokens that are long dead. The in-memory set stays the fast path: a 12h-window linear scan per request would be a self-inflicted outage on the one host that must never go down. **The health field lied in the direction that was hardest to notice.** `revocation_durable` was originally only set true *after* a successful logout, so a freshly booted system with nobody logged out reported `false` — the ordinary case crying wolf until the first logout, and then permanently true regardless of whether writes still work. The probe now performs a real create-and-remove of a `.probe` sibling, because the failure that matters is not "is the directory writable" but "can this exact path be created" — the two disagree precisely when a parent path component is a regular file, which `os.access` cheerfully reports as writable. **Two real defects surfaced while proving it, and one of them was mine.** (1) A corrupt or hand-edited file originally raised on boot; an auth store that can take the API down is worse than one that forgets, so unparseable input now degrades to "no durable revocations remembered" and logs it. (2) **The verification lied by omission.** The first draft of the corrupt-file test ended in `assert restarted.revocation_is_durable() is False or True` — a tautology that could never fail, sitting in a file whose entire purpose is refusing exactly that. It is now two real assertions (durable-capable, zero remembered revocations). Proving the claim needed a **real process kill**, not a new `AuthService()` in the same interpreter: on a spare port with its own revocation file, logout, then SIGKILL-equivalent `Stop-Process -Force`, restart, and the health block reported `revocations_tracked: 1` **at boot** — the denylist came back off disk. Replaying the revoked token then returned **`401 "session has been revoked"` with enforcement ON**, which is the assertion worth having: the first attempt at this check returned `201`, and that was *not* evidence of anything, because the spare instance had `TRADERAIG_AUTH=0` and mutations do not need a session when the door is unlocked. A control login for the same user was added so the `401` means "this token is revoked" rather than "auth is broken". **The control itself failed first, and the honest reading was the uncomfortable one.** Login returned 401, which looked exactly like the restart-logs-everyone-out bug this milestone exists to prevent. It was not that: the spare instance shared the **live** SQLite store with the 24/7 backend, and two writers on one `users` row lose each other's writes, so the credential set through the spare was overwritten. A credential silently lost to a concurrent writer is its own hazard, and the resolution is the rule this repo already has — one process, one canonical state — applied to a verification harness. Re-ran the check against an **isolated** database with the engine redirected: the password survives a full in-memory wipe and `rehydrate()`. That became a permanent test, because a credential that fails to rehydrate presents to the operator as "I typed the right password and it says invalid" on a box that reboots unattended. **That probe leaked into the live store, and cleaning it up is part of this row.** The isolation patched `persistence.db.make_live_session`, but `repositories.py` imports that name directly, so the patch did not hold and a test user landed in `.data/traderaig.db`; the same class of mistake had, earlier in the same session, set a **known throwaway password on the operator's real account**, because `POST /auth/password` sets the credential for the *acting principal* and the acting principal on `:8000` is the operator. That credential has been **cleared**, restoring the prior state of no password, and all probe users were removed out-of-band with their **audit rows deliberately preserved** — the same precedent as the row-730 verification user, because an append-only trail is the last thing to edit during an incident. The pre-existing homoglyphs at lines 157 and 309 are untouched by this row. 8 new tests (1035 -> 1043), including one whose sabotage (dropping `password_hash` in `_row_to_user`) was confirmed to turn it red, and the two durability guarantees — persistence and load — were each confirmed red when removed. Gate 6/6, **1043 passed**, live smoke 7/7. **Rejected**: a JWT/JWKS with a revocation list (rejected: the 24h/12h window is already bounded and a self-contained digest with a `jti` solves it in stdlib with no new dependency, and an external signing authority is a supply-chain surface this platform does not need yet); SQLite-backed revocation (rejected: the denylist is a high-write, disposable, read-once-per-process artifact — it belongs in a file that can be replaced atomically, and routing logouts through the same SQL store as trading state would make a file-system permission problem look like a database outage); signing or encrypting the revocation file (rejected: it contains token *identifiers* and expiries, not credentials, and encryption would buy obscurity at the cost of a key that must then be protected and rotated); reporting durability only after the first logout (rejected: see above — a field that is false in the ordinary case teaches operators to ignore it, and this project has removed more than one field that was accidentally true for the wrong reason); trusting `os.access` for the probe (rejected: it answers a different question, and answers it wrongly in the exact case the probe exists to catch) | The question this milestone answers is not "can we revoke a token" but "**is the guarantee still true after an unattended restart**", and the only way to answer that is to actually kill a process and read the file back. | 738 | 2026-10-01 | **Row 735 deferred durable baselines with an honest note, and the honest note was still wrong about who was protected**: it recorded that a restart resets every baseline and "cannot produce `ALLOW` -- the safe direction, and honest about the cost". A static-drawdown account that breached at 94,000 and recovered to 99,000 came back with no memory of it and reported its daily allowance untouched, and no amount of honest documentation makes that safe -- it is only *explained*. `HALTED` and `FAILED` behaved the same way in the mirror direction: a terminal state lost to a restart hands a failed account a clean retry, which is the single outcome the terminal states exist to prevent. Storage failure degrades rather than raises, because the asymmetry is not a judgement call: losing a write costs an account a re-declaration, raising out of the observation path takes the API down, and on a 24/7 host that converts a storage problem into an outage. `/challenge/policies` and `/challenge/status` now carry a `durability` block that says which store is in force, and a per-account `GET /challenge/accounts/{id}/baseline` exists because `/challenge/status` reports the *connected* account and "connected" is not the same question as "which account do I want to look at". **Verification needed a real process kill, and finding that out is the row's second half.** A new `ChallengeService()` in the same interpreter cannot demonstrate that the store holds anything; two tests boot a real uvicorn on a spare port and its own database file, `taskkill /F` it (not a graceful stop -- a graceful stop offers the process a chance to flush state it was never asked to flush), restart, and assert the baseline is still gradeable. **Six defects surfaced, and four of them were mine, which is now the standing pattern rather than a surprise.** (1) **`ChallengeRepository` defaulted to `bind_test_session()`** -- an in-memory SQLite with a `StaticPool`, copied from every other repository in the file, where it is the right trade for a cache you are happy to lose. The running backend would have answered `durable: true` on every status call while keeping every baseline in RAM: the control reporting itself armed with no memory at all. This is the "field true for the wrong reason" family from rows 734 and 737, and it was one line. (2) **The health field contradicted itself one line after being corrected**: `_safe()` set `_store_ok = False` on a store exception, and `_persist_baseline` set it `True` on the next line. | 773 | 2026-10-12 | **RMI-04 closes: the Research -> Strategy bridge turns a delivered Research Package into a lab-native strategy candidate, honestly and governed.** New `research/strategy_lab/research_bridge.py`: `ResearchStrategyCompiler` maps a package's own prose (title + hypothesis + method) onto one of the runtime's real detector families -- deterministically, with every default it applies named as an assumption -- and compiles it into a `StrategyDefinition` plus a **bounded** set of `StrategyVariant`s from the existing genome grid (never an unbounded search); the asset defaults to a market the cited evidence actually names; a package that cites no evidence, or names no mappable family, is refused (fail-closed, no compiling from thin air). `ResearchBindingStore` persists the package -> strategy provenance (package id, cited evidence, resolved lineage, `backed_trust`); `ResearchStrategyBridge.register` registers the flagship in the `StrategyRegistry` at **RESEARCH only** and binds the provenance -- it never promotes. `run_research_validation` runs the compiled definition through the real lab pipeline (candle -> features -> runner -> out-of-sample folds) and promotes RESEARCH -> CANDIDATE only on a passing report; VALIDATED and above stay the shared governed ReviewLoop's call. API (`operations/api.py`, all carrying `is_authorisation: False` / `trade_authority: risk/governor.py`): read-only `GET /research/strategies`, `GET /research/strategies/{id}` and `POST /research/strategies/compile` (dry run), plus governed `POST /research/strategies/register` (CAN_MANAGE_RISK) and `POST /research/strategies/{id}/validate` (CAN_TRADE). | Rationale: RMI-03 returns a Research Package, but a package is institutional reference (no family, no params, no trade permission) -- the missing RMI-04 seam is a governed, reproducible compile of that package into a lab candidate with full provenance, reusing the existing lab rather than inventing a parallel strategy path. | Compiling a package straight into a deployable strategy/rejected (bypasses the promotion ladder); mapping a package to a family with an LLM or by guessing/rejected (non-reproducible, violates the audit rule); an unbounded parameter search under a research title/rejected (the genome is the bounded, auditable DNA); promoting to VALIDATED or above in the bridge/rejected (that is the governed ReviewLoop's act alone). | **Verified:** 15 new tests in `tests/test_strategy_lab/test_research_bridge.py` (deterministic compile, evidence-derived asset, bounded variants, fail-closed on no evidence/id/title/unmappable prose, register-at-RESEARCH-only, no silent re-bind, binding + definition roundtrip); full suite **2026 passed**; ruff clean on all touched files. |

| 774 | 2026-10-12 | **RMI-05 closes: the Strategy Asset Layer gives every registered strategy durable metadata and an honest, explainable valuation -- as hooks over the existing registry, not a rewrite of it.** New `research/strategy_lab/assets.py`: `StrategyAssetMetadataStore` is a durable, keyed-by-`strategy_id` metadata journal (operator-authored owner/title/tags/arbitrary keys) that attaches **only** to a strategy already in the lab registry (fail-closed: an unregistered id is refused, never materialised); `StrategyValuationModel` is the pluggable valuation hook with a deterministic `DefaultValuationModel` that values a strategy **only from evidence that exists** -- its `ValidationReport` (pass, sample count, expected value, profit factor, Monte-Carlo survival) and its live `live_performance` -- blends them (validation 70% / live 30% when both) and scales by the governed stage (RESEARCH x0.25 ... PRODUCTION x1.0, QUARANTINED x0.0), returning a bounded `score` in [0,1], a `tier` (unproven/emerging/proven/institutional) and a named `components` breakdown; a strategy with no report and no live record is reported `unproven` with a stated basis, never a fabricated number. `StrategyAsset` is the combined view (registry entry + RMI-04 provenance + metadata + valuation). The frozen lab contracts and `StrategyRegistry` are deliberately **not** modified -- this is a hook *over* the registry, so the promotion ladder and its persistence stay exactly as the lab tests pinned. API (`operations/api.py`, all `is_authorisation: False` / `trade_authority: risk/governor.py`): read-only `GET /strategy-assets` + `GET /strategy-assets/{id}`, and governed `POST /strategy-assets/{id}/metadata` (CAN_MANAGE_RISK, attaches to a registered strategy only). | Rationale: S2's ratifying row names exactly this work ("asset metadata + valuation hooks on the existing registry"); the honest boundary is a read-mostly view keyed by strategy_id plus a pluggable valuation seam that refuses to price a strategy with no evidence, while leaving the governed registry untouched. | Adding fields to the frozen `StrategyRegistryEntry`/rejected (would churn the lab contracts and their persistence tests for no benefit; a keyed hook is the smaller, safer change); inventing a valuation with no validation/live evidence/rejected (fabrication -- the model returns `unproven`); valuing a quarantined strategy above zero/rejected (quarantine is a governed sink, worth nothing); letting this layer promote or grant a permission/rejected (promotion is ReviewLoop's act, permission is the Risk Governor's). | **Verified:** 9 new tests in `tests/test_strategy_lab/test_strategy_assets.py` (unproven-when-empty, bounded+explained valuation, stage weight, quarantined=0, live blend, metadata attach-to-registered-only, metadata persistence/merge, combined asset view, no-permission payload); 178 passed across `tests/test_strategy_lab` + the dashboard honesty guard; ruff clean. Full canonical run recorded by the gate. | |

| 775 | 2026-10-12 | **S1 Strategy Factory ships: the lab's compile → validate → promote path becomes ONE factory pipeline over the RMI-04 seam.** New `research/strategy_lab/factory.py`: `StrategySpec` is a first-class, declarative, operator/research-authored input (stable `spec_id`, family, asset, timeframe trio, trigger/exit model, min_rr, sessions, regimes, tags) with `from_dict`/`as_dict`; `StrategyFactory.compile` turns a spec into a versioned `FactoryBuild` -- a `StrategyDefinition` + a bounded variant set from the existing lab genome (`generate_variants`, capped at `MAX_VARIANTS`), content-addressed so the same spec always re-compiles to the same `variant_hash`, with every applied default named in `assumptions` and an unmappable family refused (fail-closed). `StrategyFactory.to_proposal` represents the build as the shared RMI-04 `ResearchStrategyProposal` (uniform provenance, `source: "strategy_factory"`). `build_spec` registers at **RESEARCH only** (duplicate refused) and optionally persists the spec+build in `FactorySpecStore` (durable JSON journal keyed by `strategy_id`). `run_spec` delegates to `research_bridge.run_research_validation` -- the single candle → features → runner → folds → validation engine -- so S1 and RMI-04 share one engine (no second simulator, no second gate); a passing report advances RESEARCH → CANDIDATE and nothing higher. API (`operations/api.py`, all `is_authorisation: False` / `trade_authority: risk/governor.py`): read-only `GET /strategy-factory/specs` + `GET /strategy-factory/specs/{id}`, dry-run `POST /strategy-factory/compile`, governed `POST /strategy-factory/build` (CAN_MANAGE_RISK), governed `POST /strategy-factory/{id}/validate` (CAN_TRADE). | Rationale: S1's ratifying row names exactly this work, "formalises StrategySpec → compile → validate → promote as one factory pipeline over the RMI-04 research→strategy seam". Full canonical run recorded by the gate. | |

| 782 | 2026-10-12 | **S7 Strategy NFT + Marketplace ships: a verified strategy is tokenised and can be listed and traded, with the sale settled through the one wallet ledger.** New `accounts/strategy_nft.py`. **Mint** is evidence-gated: a strategy may be tokenised only when it is at `VALIDATED` or higher (the RMI-05 valuation is good only because the promotion ladder is), has a *passing* `ValidationReport`, and has a recorded asset `owner` -- anything less is a definite refusal (`not_validated` / `no_passing_validation` / `no_owner_recorded` / `unknown_strategy`), never a token; the `token_id` is a content hash of the verified evidence `(strategy_id, family, asset, stage, report)`, so the same strategy always yields the same token and a re-mint returns it untouched (`already_minted`). **Listing** is owner-only with a bounded fixed price (`(0, MAX_LISTING_PRICE=100000]`); **buy** settles through `governance/payments.py`'s new `PaymentService.transfer(from, to, amount, source, ref)` -- a balance-checked, idempotent-by-ref move that debits the buyer and credits the seller in the same ledger the billing charges and research rewards use -- and ownership transfers only *after* the ledger settles; a repeat purchase is a `not_listed` refusal, not a second charge. A token is a *certificate of a verified strategy*, never a trade gate: no `grants_trade_permission`, every payload `is_authorisation: False` / `trade_authority: risk/governor.py` (rows 741/742). API (`operations/api.py`, 216 routes now): read-only `GET /economy/nfts` (+`?for_sale=`) and `GET /economy/nfts/{token_id}`; governed `POST /economy/nfts/mint` (`CAN_MANAGE_RISK`); owner-only `POST /economy/nfts/{token_id}/list` and `/delist`; `POST /economy/nfts/{token_id}/buy` (buyer pays their own wallet). | Rationale: S7's ratifying row (row 634) names exactly this, "strategies tokenised and listed/traded", and row 742 fixes the gate -- "the Strategy NFT is minted only off the verified Strategy Performance Ledger, never off raw statements". The honest implementation is therefore an evidence-gated mint over the RMI-05 asset (S2), plus a marketplace whose settlement is the *existing* ledger primitive, not a new money authority. | A new ledger/escrow for the marketplace/rejected (settlement is `PaymentService.transfer` on the one ledger); minting an unverified strategy/rejected (`not_validated`/`no_passing_validation`); minting without a recorded owner/rejected (`no_owner_recorded`); a duplicate mint per evidence/rejected (deterministic `token_id`); a caller-set price or payee/rejected (price from the listing, payee from the recorded owner; buyer pays own wallet); overdrawing/rejected (balance check -> 402); a self-sale/rejected (`already_owner`); an NFT granting a trade permission or entitlement/rejected (none). | **Verified:** 12 module tests in `tests/test_payments/test_strategy_nft.py` (transfer moves balance; transfer refuses overdraft/self; transfer idempotent by ref; mint requires VALIDATED+/passing report/owner; unknown refused; mint deterministic + idempotent; owner-only list + bounded price; delist requires a listing; buy settles through the wallet then transfers ownership and cannot re-charge; buy refuses overdraft and the owner buying their own token) and 5 route tests in `tests/test_operations/test_nft_routes.py` (read-only empty list; mint unknown 404; mint not-validated 409; full mint->list->403 non-owner->buy flow with wallet settlement; buy overdraft 402); 17 new tests pass; 216 routes import cleanly; 824 tests pass across the payments/operations surfaces. |  |

double-crediting a re-run/rejected (idempotent fingerprint); minting unbounded balance/rejected (`MAX_CREDIT_USD` bound); a self-service claim/rejected (governed `CAN_MANAGE_RISK`); `grants_trade_permission` on any of it/rejected (none; | 779 | 2026-10-12 | **S4 Portfolio Intelligence ships: the cross-strategy Capital Allocation Engine turns the existing aggregation into an advisory target book -- risk-parity weights, correlation-damped, cap-clamped, and honest when evidence is thin.** New `portfolio/allocation.py`: `PortfolioAllocationEngine.propose(method="risk_parity")` reads the same realised-PnL series `portfolio.correlation` uses (`_pnl_series`) and the same durable caps `CapitalAllocationBoard` holds -- it is an *engine over* the existing pieces, not a new authority (no orders, no cap mutation) -- and returns an advisory `AllocationProposal`; base weights are inverse-volatility (risk parity) or equal-weight, each strategy's adjusted weight is divided by `1 + 0.5 × (concentrated pairs it participates in)` so a strategy that doubles up is damped, and a bounded water-filling pass clamps every weight to its strategy cap (institutional default 0.20) and holds any unallocated remainder as `cash`; a strategy with fewer than `MIN_SAMPLES` samples or zero volatility is never sized (reported under `insufficient_data`), and with no eligible strategy the proposal is `unavailable` with empty weights -- no fabricated number. ### M8 — Live MT5 execution (operational)
- [x] Hard gates: MT5_LIVE_TRADING arm flag (default off); live start refused without explicit consent
- [x] Promotion-cleared requirement: only strategies at stage paper or production allowed; registry read live
- [x] Strategy linkage: adapter enforces 
equire_strategy_linkage=True (candidates without strategy_id vetoed before order_send)
- [x] Mutual exclusion: starting live stops paper; starting paper stops live (one desk)
- [x] API: GET /mt5/engine (read-only status/ready/refusals), POST /mt5/engine/start|stop (CAN_TRADE gated)
- [x] Engine-internal checks: LiveEngine.start() re-validates gates (fail-closed)
- [x] Identity/entitlement mapping: account linked to user; challenge binding active
- [x] Guardrail tests: live arming, linkage gate, API routes
- [ ] Production-only docs: UPDATE.md Appendix A + PROJECT_MAP milestone entries (live MT5 endpoints + semantics)
